+/// The files `mod name;` at `scope` in the file at `path` can load: `name.rs`
+/// or `name/mod.rs`, inside each inline module around it, under the declaring
+/// file's own directory (a `mod.rs` or a crate root such as `lib.rs`) or, for
+/// any other file, the directory named for it. `None` when an inline module
+/// around it is itself conditional, which may carry a `#[path]` that moves it.
+fn module_files(
+    path: &Path,
+    file: &FileImports,
+    (scope, name): &(usize, String),
+) -> Option<Vec<PathBuf>> {
+    let mut inline = Vec::new();
+    let mut at = Some(*scope);
+    while let Some(scope) = at {
+        match file.modules.get(&scope) {
+            Some(declaration) => {
+                if !file.declared.contains(declaration) {
+                    return None;
+                }
+                inline.push(declaration.1.clone());
+                at = Some(declaration.0);
+            }
+            None => at = file.parent[scope],
+        }
+    }
+    let dir = path.parent().unwrap_or(Path::new(""));
+    let mut bases = vec![dir.to_path_buf()];
+    if path.file_name() != Some("mod.rs".as_ref()) {
+        bases.extend(path.file_stem().map(|stem| dir.join(stem)));
     }
+    Some(
+        bases
+            .into_iter()
+            .flat_map(|base| {
+                let dir = inline
+                    .iter()
+                    .rev()
+                    .fold(base, |dir, module| dir.join(module));
+                [
+                    dir.join(format!("{name}.rs")),
+                    dir.join(name).join("mod.rs"),
+                ]
+            })
+            .collect(),
+    )
 }

## Python Package Management
- **Default to `uv add <pkg>` for adding Python dependencies** — never `pip install` into a uv-managed project. `uv add` updates `pyproject.toml` and `uv.lock` atomically.
- Use `uv add --dev <pkg>` for dev-only deps (pytest, ruff, mypy), `uv remove <pkg>` to drop, and `uv sync` to install the locked set.
- Run tools via `uv run <cmd>` (e.g. `uv run pytest`, `uv run ruff check`) so they execute inside the project's venv without manual activation.
- Only fall back to `pip` for repos that explicitly use a different manager (Poetry, plain venv + requirements.txt) — detect by presence of `uv.lock`/`poetry.lock`/etc. before choosing.
     1	//! AC-1's static half: the room reaches no generator, verifier or LLM code.
     2	//!
     3	//! Generation, verification and every model call live in the offline pipeline
     4	//! (`pipeline/`, the Python package `popquiz`). The room must have no way to
     5	//! call into any of it. This file reads the room's manifest, its lock and its
     6	//! source, and refuses:
     7	//!
     8	//! - a dependency in any table (ordinary, optional, feature-gated, dev, build,
     9	//!   per-target, patch, replace) whose crate name is denied, or whose `path`
    10	//!   points into `pipeline/`;
    11	//! - a denied crate anywhere in the lock's subgraph under `room`;
    12	//! - a `use` or `extern crate` in `src/` whose root is a denied name.
    13	//!
    14	//! **The denylist names crates, never folders.** The room's own folder is
    15	//! `popquiz/`, the same word as the Python package, so a check on paths would
    16	//! fail on main or need a carve-out. A dependency carries a name, and names are
    17	//! what is checked; the only path check resolves a `path` dependency against
    18	//! the repository's `pipeline/` folder.
    19	//!
    20	//! The manifest and the lock are read by a small TOML parser and the source by
    21	//! a small Rust tokenizer, both below, so layout (dotted or quoted keys, escaped
    22	//! strings, comments, imports split across lines) does not change what is seen.
    23	//! No crate is added for either.
    24	//!
    25	//! A scan is a tripwire, not a proof: someone who means to can dodge it. Imports
    26	//! a macro writes, and paths used with no `use` at all (`openai::Client::new()`),
    27	//! are left to the manifest and lock checks: a crate the source can name must be
    28	//! declared there first. This is the static half of AC-1 only.
    29	
    30	use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};
    31	use std::fs;
    32	use std::path::{Component, Path, PathBuf};
    33	
    34	// --- The denylist -------------------------------------------------------------
    35	
    36	/// Words that deny a crate when one of them is a whole `_`-separated part of
    37	/// its name: `async-openai` and `cargo-miri` are denied, `generic-array` is not.
    38	/// `popquiz` is the pipeline's Python package (its import and distribution name
    39	/// in `pipeline/pyproject.toml`); `pipeline` is the folder that holds it.
    40	const DENIED_WORDS: &[&str] = &[
    41	    "anthropic",
    42	    "openai",
    43	    "llm",
    44	    "generator",
    45	    "miri",
    46	    "pipeline",
    47	    "popquiz",
    48	];
    49	
    50	/// The compiler's own crates (`rustc_driver`, `rustc_interface`, …), which is
    51	/// how Miri or an in-process verifier would reach rustc. This also refuses
    52	/// crates.io's `rustc-hash` and `rustc_version`. Neither is in the lock today;
    53	/// adding one is a person's call, not a reason to loosen this.
    54	const DENIED_PREFIX: &str = "rustc_";
    55	
    56	/// Whether a crate name is denied. Cargo treats `-` and `_` in a name as the
    57	/// same character, so this does too.
    58	fn denied(name: &str) -> bool {
    59	    let name = name.trim().to_ascii_lowercase().replace('-', "_");
    60	    name.starts_with(DENIED_PREFIX) || name.split('_').any(|word| DENIED_WORDS.contains(&word))
    61	}
    62	
    63	const WHY: &str = "AC-1: the room must not reach generator, verifier or LLM code \
    64	                   (popquiz/tests/dependencies.rs)";
    65	
    66	// --- Files --------------------------------------------------------------------
    67	
    68	/// The room crate's folder. Read at run time, so the built test can be pointed
    69	/// at a throwaway copy holding a planted dependency that `cargo --locked`
    70	/// would refuse to build. `cargo test` sets it; the compile-time value is the
    71	/// fallback.
    72	fn crate_dir() -> PathBuf {
    73	    std::env::var_os("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")
    74	        .map(PathBuf::from)
    75	        .unwrap_or_else(|| PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")))
    76	}
    77	
    78	fn read(path: &Path) -> String {
    79	    fs::read_to_string(path).unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("read {}: {e}", path.display()))
    80	}
    81	
    82	fn rust_files(dir: &Path, out: &mut Vec<PathBuf>) {
    83	    let mut entries: Vec<PathBuf> = fs::read_dir(dir)
    84	        .unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("read {}: {e}", dir.display()))
    85	        .map(|entry| entry.expect("directory entry").path())
    86	        .collect();
    87	    entries.sort();
    88	    for path in entries {
    89	        if path.is_dir() {
    90	            rust_files(&path, out);
    91	        } else if path.extension().is_some_and(|ext| ext == "rs") {
    92	            out.push(path);
    93	        }
    94	    }
    95	}
    96	
    97	/// `a/b/../c` as `a/c`, without touching the filesystem.
    98	fn normalize(path: &Path) -> PathBuf {
    99	    let mut out = PathBuf::new();
   100	    for component in path.components() {
   101	        match component {
   102	            Component::ParentDir => {
   103	                out.pop();
   104	            }
   105	            Component::CurDir => {}
   106	            other => out.push(other.as_os_str()),
   107	        }
   108	    }
   109	    out
   110	}
   111	
   112	// --- TOML -----------------------------------------------------------------------
   113	//
   114	// Enough of TOML 1.0 for Cargo.toml and Cargo.lock: bare, quoted and dotted
   115	// keys; `[table]` and `[[array]]` headers; the four string kinds with their
   116	// escapes; arrays and inline tables. Numbers, booleans and dates are kept as
   117	// `Other`, since nothing here reads them. Malformed input panics with a line.
   118	
   119	#[derive(Debug)]
   120	enum Toml {
   121	    Str(String),
   122	    Table(Vec<(String, Toml)>),
   123	    Array(Vec<Toml>),
   124	    Other,
   125	}
   126	
   127	impl Toml {
   128	    fn get(&self, key: &str) -> Option<&Toml> {
   129	        self.entries()
   130	            .iter()
   131	            .find(|(name, _)| name == key)
   132	            .map(|(_, value)| value)
   133	    }
   134	
   135	    fn str(&self) -> Option<&str> {
   136	        match self {
   137	            Toml::Str(text) => Some(text),
   138	            _ => None,
   139	        }
   140	    }
   141	
   142	    fn entries(&self) -> &[(String, Toml)] {
   143	        match self {
   144	            Toml::Table(entries) => entries,
   145	            _ => &[],
   146	        }
   147	    }
   148	
   149	    fn items(&self) -> &[Toml] {
   150	        match self {
   151	            Toml::Array(items) => items,
   152	            _ => &[],
   153	        }
   154	    }
   155	}
   156	
   157	/// The table a key path names, created where missing. A path through an array
   158	/// of tables goes into its last element, as a TOML header does.
   159	fn table_at<'a>(
   160	    table: &'a mut Vec<(String, Toml)>,
   161	    path: &[String],
   162	) -> &'a mut Vec<(String, Toml)> {
   163	    let Some((first, rest)) = path.split_first() else {
   164	        return table;
   165	    };
   166	    let index = match table.iter().position(|(name, _)| name == first) {
   167	        Some(index) => index,
   168	        None => {
   169	            table.push((first.clone(), Toml::Table(Vec::new())));
   170	            table.len() - 1
   171	        }
   172	    };
   173	    let child = match &mut table[index].1 {
   174	        Toml::Table(entries) => entries,
   175	        Toml::Array(items) => match items.last_mut() {
   176	            Some(Toml::Table(entries)) => entries,
   177	            _ => panic!("TOML key `{first}` is an array, not an array of tables"),
   178	        },
   179	        _ => panic!("TOML key `{first}` is a value, not a table"),
   180	    };
   181	    table_at(child, rest)
   182	}
   183	
   184	fn insert(table: &mut Vec<(String, Toml)>, key: &[String], value: Toml) {
   185	    let (last, parents) = key.split_last().expect("a key has at least one part");
   186	    table_at(table, parents).push((last.clone(), value));
   187	}
   188	
   189	struct TomlParser {
   190	    text: Vec<char>,
   191	    at: usize,
   192	}
   193	
   194	impl TomlParser {
   195	    fn peek(&self) -> Option<char> {
   196	        self.text.get(self.at).copied()
   197	    }
   198	
   199	    fn looking_at(&self, word: &str) -> bool {
   200	        word.chars()
   201	            .enumerate()
   202	            .all(|(k, c)| self.text.get(self.at + k) == Some(&c))
   203	    }
   204	
   205	    fn fail(&self, what: &str) -> ! {
   206	        let end = self.at.min(self.text.len());
   207	        let line = self.text[..end].iter().filter(|c| **c == '\n').count() + 1;
   208	        panic!("TOML line {line}: {what}")
   209	    }
   210	
   211	    fn expect(&mut self, c: char) {
   212	        if self.peek() != Some(c) {
   213	            self.fail(&format!("expected `{c}`"));
   214	        }
   215	        self.at += 1;
   216	    }
   217	
   218	    fn spaces(&mut self) {
   219	        while matches!(self.peek(), Some(' ' | '\t')) {
   220	            self.at += 1;
   221	        }
   222	    }
   223	
   224	    /// Spaces, line breaks and comments.
   225	    fn blank(&mut self) {
   226	        loop {
   227	            self.spaces();
   228	            match self.peek() {
   229	                Some('\n' | '\r') => self.at += 1,
   230	                Some('#') => {
   231	                    while !matches!(self.peek(), None | Some('\n')) {
   232	                        self.at += 1;
   233	                    }
   234	                }
   235	                _ => return,
   236	            }
   237	        }
   238	    }
   239	
   240	    fn key(&mut self) -> Vec<String> {
   241	        let mut parts = Vec::new();
   242	        loop {
   243	            self.spaces();
   244	            let part = match self.peek() {
   245	                Some('"') => self.basic_string(),
   246	                Some('\'') => self.literal_string(),
   247	                _ => {
   248	                    let start = self.at;
   249	                    while self
   250	                        .peek()
   251	                        .is_some_and(|c| c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || c == '_' || c == '-')
   252	                    {
   253	                        self.at += 1;
   254	                    }
   255	                    if start == self.at {
   256	                        self.fail("expected a key");
   257	                    }
   258	                    self.text[start..self.at].iter().collect()
   259	                }
   260	            };
   261	            parts.push(part);
   262	            self.spaces();
   263	            if self.peek() != Some('.') {
   264	                return parts;
   265	            }
   266	            self.at += 1;
   267	        }
   268	    }
   269	
   270	    /// The end of a multi-line string: three quotes, and up to two more that
   271	    /// belong to the content.
   272	    fn closes_multiline(&mut self, quote: char, out: &mut String) -> bool {
   273	        let mut run = 0;
   274	        while self.text.get(self.at + run) == Some(&quote) {
   275	            run += 1;
   276	        }
   277	        if run < 3 {
   278	            return false;
   279	        }
   280	        let extra = (run - 3).min(2);
   281	        out.extend(std::iter::repeat_n(quote, extra));
   282	        self.at += 3 + extra;
   283	        true
   284	    }
   285	
   286	    fn skip_first_newline(&mut self) {
   287	        if self.looking_at("\r\n") {
   288	            self.at += 2;
   289	        } else if self.looking_at("\n") {
   290	            self.at += 1;
   291	        }
   292	    }
   293	
   294	    fn basic_string(&mut self) -> String {
   295	        let multi = self.looking_at("\"\"\"");
   296	        self.at += if multi { 3 } else { 1 };
   297	        if multi {
   298	            self.skip_first_newline();
   299	        }
   300	        let mut out = String::new();
   301	        loop {
   302	            let Some(c) = self.peek() else {
   303	                self.fail("unterminated string");
   304	            };
   305	            if multi && c == '"' && self.closes_multiline('"', &mut out) {
   306	                return out;
   307	            }
   308	            if !multi && c == '"' {
   309	                self.at += 1;
   310	                return out;
   311	            }
   312	            if !multi && c == '\n' {
   313	                self.fail("line break in a one-line string");
   314	            }
   315	            self.at += 1;
   316	            if c != '\\' {
   317	                out.push(c);
   318	                continue;
   319	            }
   320	            let Some(escape) = self.peek() else {
   321	                self.fail("unterminated escape");
   322	            };
   323	            self.at += 1;
   324	            match escape {
   325	                'b' => out.push('\u{8}'),
   326	                't' => out.push('\t'),
   327	                'n' => out.push('\n'),
   328	                'f' => out.push('\u{c}'),
   329	                'r' => out.push('\r'),
   330	                'e' => out.push('\u{1b}'),
   331	                '"' => out.push('"'),
   332	                '\\' => out.push('\\'),
   333	                'u' | 'U' | 'x' => {
   334	                    let width = match escape {
   335	                        'u' => 4,
   336	                        'U' => 8,
   337	                        _ => 2,
   338	                    };
   339	                    let Some(hex) = self.text.get(self.at..self.at + width) else {
   340	                        self.fail("short unicode escape");
   341	                    };
   342	                    let hex: String = hex.iter().collect();
   343	                    let Some(decoded) = u32::from_str_radix(&hex, 16).ok().and_then(char::from_u32)
   344	                    else {
   345	                        self.fail("bad unicode escape");
   346	                    };
   347	                    out.push(decoded);
   348	                    self.at += width;
   349	                }
   350	                ' ' | '\t' | '\n' | '\r' if multi => {
   351	                    // A line-ending backslash: the break and the next line's indent go.
   352	                    while matches!(self.peek(), Some(' ' | '\t' | '\n' | '\r')) {
   353	                        self.at += 1;
   354	                    }
   355	                }
   356	                _ => self.fail("bad escape"),
   357	            }
   358	        }
   359	    }
   360	
   361	    fn literal_string(&mut self) -> String {
   362	        let multi = self.looking_at("'''");
   363	        self.at += if multi { 3 } else { 1 };
   364	        if multi {
   365	            self.skip_first_newline();
   366	        }
   367	        let mut out = String::new();
   368	        loop {
   369	            let Some(c) = self.peek() else {
   370	                self.fail("unterminated string");
   371	            };
   372	            if multi && c == '\'' && self.closes_multiline('\'', &mut out) {
   373	                return out;
   374	            }
   375	            if !multi && c == '\'' {
   376	                self.at += 1;
   377	                return out;
   378	            }
   379	            if !multi && c == '\n' {
   380	                self.fail("line break in a one-line string");
   381	            }
   382	            out.push(c);
   383	            self.at += 1;
   384	        }
   385	    }
   386	
   387	    fn value(&mut self) -> Toml {
   388	        match self.peek() {
   389	            Some('"') => Toml::Str(self.basic_string()),
   390	            Some('\'') => Toml::Str(self.literal_string()),
   391	            Some('[') => {
   392	                self.at += 1;
   393	                let mut items = Vec::new();
   394	                loop {
   395	                    self.blank();
   396	                    if self.peek() == Some(']') {
   397	                        self.at += 1;
   398	                        return Toml::Array(items);
   399	                    }
   400	                    items.push(self.value());
   401	                    self.blank();
   402	                    match self.peek() {
   403	                        Some(',') => self.at += 1,
   404	                        Some(']') => {}
   405	                        _ => self.fail("expected `,` or `]`"),
   406	                    }
   407	                }
   408	            }
   409	            Some('{') => {
   410	                self.at += 1;
   411	                let mut table = Vec::new();
   412	                loop {
   413	                    self.blank();
   414	                    if self.peek() == Some('}') {
   415	                        self.at += 1;
   416	                        return Toml::Table(table);
   417	                    }
   418	                    let key = self.key();
   419	                    self.expect('=');
   420	                    self.spaces();
   421	                    let value = self.value();
   422	                    insert(&mut table, &key, value);
   423	                    self.blank();
   424	                    match self.peek() {
   425	                        Some(',') => self.at += 1,
   426	                        Some('}') => {}
   427	                        _ => self.fail("expected `,` or `}`"),
   428	                    }
   429	                }
   430	            }
   431	            Some(_) => {
   432	                let start = self.at;
   433	                while !matches!(
   434	                    self.peek(),
   435	                    None | Some(',' | ']' | '}' | '\n' | '\r' | '#')
   436	                ) {
   437	                    self.at += 1;
   438	                }
   439	                if start == self.at {
   440	                    self.fail("expected a value");
   441	                }
   442	                Toml::Other
   443	            }
   444	            None => self.fail("expected a value"),
   445	        }
   446	    }
   447	}
   448	
   449	fn parse_toml(text: &str) -> Toml {
   450	    let mut parser = TomlParser {
   451	        text: text.chars().collect(),
   452	        at: 0,
   453	    };
   454	    let mut root = Vec::new();
   455	    let mut current: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   456	    loop {
   457	        parser.blank();
   458	        match parser.peek() {
   459	            None => return Toml::Table(root),
   460	            Some('[') => {
   461	                parser.at += 1;
   462	                let array = parser.peek() == Some('[');
   463	                if array {
   464	                    parser.at += 1;
   465	                }
   466	                let key = parser.key();
   467	                parser.expect(']');
   468	                if array {
   469	                    parser.expect(']');
   470	                    let (last, parents) = key.split_last().expect("a header has a key");
   471	                    let parent = table_at(&mut root, parents);
   472	                    match parent.iter_mut().find(|(name, _)| name == last) {
   473	                        Some((_, Toml::Array(items))) => items.push(Toml::Table(Vec::new())),
   474	                        Some(_) => parser.fail("`[[…]]` over a key that is not an array"),
   475	                        None => {
   476	                            parent.push((last.clone(), Toml::Array(vec![Toml::Table(Vec::new())])))
   477	                        }
   478	                    }
   479	                } else {
   480	                    table_at(&mut root, &key);
   481	                }
   482	                current = key;
   483	            }
   484	            Some(_) => {
   485	                let key = parser.key();
   486	                parser.expect('=');
   487	                parser.spaces();
   488	                let value = parser.value();
   489	                insert(table_at(&mut root, &current), &key, value);
   490	            }
   491	        }
   492	        parser.spaces();
   493	        if !matches!(parser.peek(), None | Some('\n' | '\r' | '#')) {
   494	            parser.fail("expected the end of the line");
   495	        }
   496	    }
   497	}
   498	
   499	// --- The manifest -------------------------------------------------------------
   500	
   501	/// The tables a dependency can be declared in, at the root or under
   502	/// `target.<cfg>`. Cargo also takes the `_` spellings.
   503	const DEPENDENCY_KINDS: &[&str] = &[
   504	    "dependencies",
   505	    "dev-dependencies",
   506	    "dev_dependencies",
   507	    "build-dependencies",
   508	    "build_dependencies",
   509	];
   510	
   511	/// Every root key Cargo.toml may hold, so a section this file does not read
   512	/// fails `the_readers_see_every_section_they_check` instead of passing unread.
   513	const KNOWN_ROOT_KEYS: &[&str] = &[
   514	    "cargo-features",
   515	    "package",
   516	    "lib",
   517	    "bin",
   518	    "example",
   519	    "test",
   520	    "bench",
   521	    "features",
   522	    "dependencies",
   523	    "dev-dependencies",
   524	    "dev_dependencies",
   525	    "build-dependencies",
   526	    "build_dependencies",
   527	    "target",
   528	    "patch",
   529	    "replace",
   530	    "badges",
[package]
name = "room"
version = "0.1.0"
edition = "2021"
publish = false
description = "The live room: one meetup, one question, seven phases."

# Bins are listed explicitly rather than discovered, so that `src/cli/` can hold
# a shared module (`spike_shared.rs`) that is not itself a binary. The answer
# fingerprint in it is computed on both sides of the wire; a second copy that
# could drift would quietly weaken the AC-52 check into an aggregate one.
autobins = false

# This crate is built with whatever `cargo` the machine has. It is deliberately
# not pinned: the verification toolchain pin (rustc -Vv release and commit-hash,
# and the nightly's date for Miri) is a different thing entirely, defined once
# by T-15a where the sandbox image is built. See SPEC.md 7.2 and BUILDPLAN D-17.

[[bin]]
name = "room"
path = "src/server/main.rs"

# --- The T-03 burst spike. Throwaway, frozen; both bins are gated off. --------
#
# `required-features` is what holds EVALUATION.md's 60 s budget for `just test`:
# `cargo test --offline --locked` passes no `--features`, so neither bin and not
# one line of the crates below is compiled by the inner loop. The budget is kept
# by construction rather than by care.
#
# T-21 renamed the spike's client `spike-burst` (the room's own `burst` is
# below) and kept both bins so the committed reports in `spike/reports/` stay
# reproducible. `just test-full` type-checks and unit-tests them (`harness-full`).

[[bin]]
name = "spike-server"
path = "src/cli/spike-server.rs"
required-features = ["spike"]

[[bin]]
name = "spike-burst"
path = "src/cli/spike-burst.rs"
required-features = ["spike"]

# --- T-09: the deployed room's end-to-end check. ------------------------------
#
# `just smoke <url>` runs it against a room over https/wss (the Fly edge) or
# plain http/ws (loopback). Gated like the spike bins, for the same reason: the
# TLS client is compiled by nothing in `just test`.

[[bin]]
name = "smoke"
path = "src/cli/smoke.rs"
required-features = ["smoke"]

# --- T-21: the room under load (`just burst <url>`). ---------------------------
#
# Smoke's client (`src/cli/room_client.rs`, shared) with the spike's
# measurement (`src/cli/burst_report.rs`, which `tests/burst_report.rs` checks
# inside `just test` — it is pure, so it needs no feature). Gated like smoke.

[[bin]]
name = "burst"
path = "src/cli/burst.rs"
required-features = ["burst"]

# The in-process room that `test-full` points burst and smoke at (T-21): the
# real router on loopback, authorized by the tests' `HostAuth`. A test, so

# (`host/discord/http.rs`) rides rustls with ring, tokio-rustls and Mozilla's
# roots — all three were in the lock already (the spike and smoke used them as
# optional dependencies), so no crate was added. Default features off so there
# is no second crypto provider; `host/discord/http.rs` passes ring to its

# Rust NYC Pop Quiz — the harness.
#
# The eight recipe names below are reserved by EVALUATION.md's harness table and
# mean the same thing everywhere in the contract. Those whose suites do not exist
# yet (PENDING) print which ticket delivers them and fail, so that a suite can
# never look green by not being there.
#
#   just setup          fetch dependencies
#   just test           the inner loop: hermetic, parallel, offline, <= 60 s
#   just test-full      everything that exists, plus what is still pending
#   just sandbox-build  build the verification image from pin.toml (T-15a)
#   just secret-scan    AC-101's static half: no admin token in the repository (T-25)
#   just a11y           AC-82…AC-86 on every surface and phase, and the matrix (T-13)
#   just smoke URL      the deployed room end to end, all seven phases (T-09)
#   just burst URL      AC-54, AC-53, AC-41 and AC-52 under 200 participants (T-21)
#
# Why `setup` exists: `test` has to be hermetic, and a fresh .venv has to come
# from somewhere. Splitting the network step out is what lets `test` be run
# with --offline and held to it, rather than merely asked not to reach out.
#
# Two recipes use the network, and neither is reachable from `test`: `setup`, and
# `sandbox-build`, which downloads a toolchain into an image. `sandbox-build` is a
# prerequisite of `test-full` only. The image it produces runs with no network at
# all, which is why the toolchain has to be baked in at build time.

# Suites not built yet, and the ticket that delivers each (`name:T-nn`, space
# separated). `test-full` prints them. Empty since T-21 built `burst`; the list
# stays for the next reserved name that lands before its suite.
PENDING := ""

# Secrets for the recipes that need one (`generate`, `schedule`, `sync`, `smoke`,
# `burst`) come from 1Password: `.env.op` holds op:// references, never values,
# and `op run` resolves them into the environment of that one subprocess. Sign
# in first (`op signin`, or the app's CLI integration). A value already exported
# in the shell is overridden by the reference, so 1Password is the only source
# here; `demo` keeps its own `op read` fallback. Every reference in `.env.op`
# must resolve, whichever recipe runs: a missing item fails all five.
op_run := "op run --env-file='" + justfile_directory() + "/.env.op' --"

_default:
    @just --list --unsorted

# Fetch every dependency. The only recipe that touches the network.
setup:
    cd pipeline && uv sync --frozen
    cd popquiz && cargo fetch --locked
    cd prototypes/organizer-tui && cargo fetch --locked

# The inner loop. No network, no Docker, no pinned toolchain, no Miri.
# EVALUATION.md budgets this at 60 s wall-clock; a slower default suite is a defect.
[parallel]
test: test-room test-pipeline test-web test-organizer

# Checks formatting (popquiz/rustfmt.toml), then builds and tests the room crate with whatever cargo this machine has.
test-room:
    cd popquiz && cargo fmt --check
    cd popquiz && cargo test --offline --locked

# Organizer UI stays checked against the room API it imports.
test-organizer:
    cd prototypes/organizer-tui && cargo test --offline --locked
    cd prototypes/organizer-tui && cargo clippy --offline --locked --all-targets -- -D warnings
    cd prototypes/organizer-tui && cargo fmt --check
    cd prototypes/organizer-tui && cargo build --offline --locked
    python3 prototypes/organizer-tui/tests/terminal_repeat.py
    python3 -m unittest discover -s prototypes/organizer-tui/tests -p 'test_terminal_repeat.py'

# The pipeline's decision logic, against StubRunner's recorded outputs.
#
# tests/sandbox is ignored here and nowhere else: it is the AC-12 containment suite
# and it needs a real container, which this hook is forbidden from having. Ignoring
# the directory means its conftest is never even imported — and that conftest
# refuses to run unasked, so the suite has no way to look green without a container.
test-pipeline:
    cd pipeline && uv run --offline --no-sync pytest --ignore=tests/sandbox

# Node's built-in runner. No package.json, no node_modules, nothing to install.
# The glob is quoted so node expands it, not the shell: with no package.json
# anywhere, node resolves a bare directory argument as a module specifier and
# fails looking for an index, rather than scanning it for tests.
test-web:
    node --test 'web/test/*.test.js'

# `a11y` is not a dependency: `test` already runs web/test/a11y.test.js (the
# test-web glob); `just a11y` is the same suite with its matrix printed.
#
# Everything that exists today, then an honest list of what does not.
# Green by contract: the pending suites are named here, never invoked here.
test-full: test sandbox-build test-sandbox test-verify-full test-generate-full test-transport-full (canary "--full-only") harness-full
    #!/usr/bin/env bash
    set -euo pipefail
    (cd popquiz && cargo test --offline --locked --test lifecycle -- --ignored test_full_)  # T-10: AC-69 on the Discord mock

    echo "Ran: test (room, pipeline, web), the canary over real sockets, the AC-12"
    echo "     containment suite, the verifier's cases on the sandbox image, the"
    echo "     transport at 200 buzzers, and burst (200 participants) and smoke"
    echo "     against an in-process room on loopback (T-21; numbers labelled loopback)."
    echo ""
    echo "PENDING — these suites are not built yet:"
    pending=0
    for entry in {{ PENDING }}; do
        printf '  %-12s delivered by %s\n' "${entry%%:*}" "${entry##*:}"
        pending=1
    done
    [ "$pending" = 1 ] || echo "  (none)"
    echo ""
    echo "Run in a browser, not here — no headless browser on this machine or in CI:"
    echo "  wall-layout  the wall's measured layout, AC-100 / AC-33 (just wall-layout)"
    echo "  fallback     the static fallback stepped offline, AC-102 (popquiz/README.md, Pages)"

   535	
   536	/// One dependency as the manifest declares it.
   537	struct Dep {
   538	    /// Where it is declared, as a dotted table name.
   539	    table: String,
   540	    /// The key it is declared under (for `[replace]`, the name before `:`).
   541	    name: String,
   542	    /// `package = "…"`: the crate it really is, when renamed.
   543	    package: Option<String>,
   544	    path: Option<String>,
   545	    /// `workspace = true`: the real spec is the workspace's entry of this name.
   546	    /// (Cargo refuses `workspace = false`, so the key alone says it.)
   547	    inherited: bool,
   548	}
   549	
   550	/// The crate a `[replace]` key names. Cargo takes a package ID spec there:
   551	/// `name`, `name:version` or `name@version`, optionally after a source URL and
   552	/// `#`; a URL whose fragment is only a version (`url#1.0.0`), or that has no
   553	/// fragment, names the last segment of its path.
   554	fn spec_name(spec: &str) -> String {
   555	    let (url, rest) = match spec.split_once('#') {
   556	        Some((url, rest)) => (Some(url), rest),
   557	        None if spec.contains("://") => (Some(spec), ""),
   558	        None => (None, spec),
   559	    };
   560	    let rest = if url.is_some() {
   561	        url_text(rest)
   562	    } else {
   563	        rest.to_string()
   564	    };
   565	    let name = rest.split(['@', ':']).next().unwrap_or_default();
   566	    match url {
   567	        Some(url) if name.is_empty() || name.starts_with(|c: char| c.is_ascii_digit()) => {
   568	            let last = url_path_segments(url).pop().unwrap_or_default();
   569	            last.strip_suffix(".git").unwrap_or(&last).to_string()
   570	        }
   571	        _ => name.to_string(),
   572	    }
   573	}
   574	
   575	/// A URL as the WHATWG parser Cargo uses reads it: tabs and newlines anywhere
   576	/// are dropped, and leading or trailing spaces and control characters trimmed.
   577	fn url_text(url: &str) -> String {
   578	    url.trim_matches(|c: char| c <= ' ')
   579	        .chars()
   580	        .filter(|c| !matches!(c, '\t' | '\n' | '\r'))
   581	        .collect()
   582	}
   583	
   584	/// The path segments of a URL as that parser leaves them: `\` separates them
   585	/// like `/`, `.` segments (also written `%2e`) are dropped and `..` ones remove
   586	/// the segment before, and a trailing `/` adds no segment of its own.
   587	fn url_path_segments(url: &str) -> Vec<String> {
   588	    let url = url_text(url).replace('\\', "/");
   589	    let url = url.split(['?', '#']).next().unwrap_or_default();
   590	    let path = url.split_once("://").map_or(url, |(_, rest)| {
   591	        rest.split_once('/').map_or("", |(_, path)| path)
   592	    });
   593	    let mut segments: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   594	    for segment in path.split('/') {
   595	        match segment.to_ascii_lowercase().replace("%2e", ".").as_str() {
   596	            "" | "." => {}
   597	            ".." => {
   598	                segments.pop();
   599	            }
   600	            _ => segments.push(segment.to_string()),
   601	        }
   602	    }
   603	    segments
   604	}
   605	
   606	/// Where inherited dependency specs live. The room is its own workspace root,
   607	/// so this is in its own manifest.
   608	const WORKSPACE_DEPENDENCIES: &str = "workspace.dependencies";
   609	
   610	/// Every dependency in every dependency table, the workspace's included.
   611	fn manifest_deps(manifest: &Toml) -> Vec<Dep> {
   612	    let mut deps = Vec::new();
   613	    let mut read_table = |table: String, entries: &[(String, Toml)]| {
   614	        for (key, spec) in entries {
   615	            let field = |name: &str| spec.get(name).and_then(Toml::str).map(String::from);
   616	            deps.push(Dep {
   617	                table: table.clone(),
   618	                name: if table == "replace" {
   619	                    spec_name(key)
   620	                } else {
   621	                    key.clone()
   622	                },
   623	                package: field("package"),
   624	                path: field("path"),
   625	                inherited: spec.get("workspace").is_some(),
   626	            });
   627	        }
   628	    };
   629	    if let Some(table) = manifest
   630	        .get("workspace")
   631	        .and_then(|workspace| workspace.get("dependencies"))
   632	    {
   633	        read_table(WORKSPACE_DEPENDENCIES.to_string(), table.entries());
   634	    }
   635	    for kind in DEPENDENCY_KINDS {
   636	        if let Some(table) = manifest.get(kind) {
   637	            read_table(kind.to_string(), table.entries());
   638	        }
   639	    }
   640	    for (cfg, target) in manifest.get("target").map_or(&[][..], Toml::entries) {
   641	        for kind in DEPENDENCY_KINDS {
   642	            if let Some(table) = target.get(kind) {
   643	                read_table(format!("target.{cfg}.{kind}"), table.entries());
   644	            }
   645	        }
   646	    }
   647	    for (source, table) in manifest.get("patch").map_or(&[][..], Toml::entries) {
   648	        read_table(format!("patch.{source}"), table.entries());
   649	    }
   650	    if let Some(table) = manifest.get("replace") {
   651	        read_table("replace".to_string(), table.entries());
   652	    }
   653	    deps
   654	}
   655	
   656	/// What is wrong with a manifest that sits in `crate_dir`.
   657	fn manifest_violations(crate_dir: &Path, manifest: &str) -> Vec<String> {
   658	    let pipeline = normalize(&crate_dir.join("..").join("pipeline"));
   659	    let mut violations = Vec::new();
   660	    let deps = manifest_deps(&parse_toml(manifest));
   661	    let in_workspace: BTreeSet<&str> = deps
   662	        .iter()
   663	        .filter(|dep| dep.table == WORKSPACE_DEPENDENCIES)
   664	        .map(|dep| dep.name.as_str())
   665	        .collect();
   666	    for dep in &deps {
   667	        let at = format!("Cargo.toml [{}] {}", dep.table, dep.name);
   668	        // Its spec is checked where it is written. One this manifest does not
   669	        // write comes from a workspace above the room that this test cannot read.
   670	        if dep.inherited && !in_workspace.contains(dep.name.as_str()) {
   671	            violations.push(format!(
   672	                "{at}: inherits from a workspace outside the room's manifest"
   673	            ));
   674	        }
   675	    }
   676	    for dep in deps {
   677	        let at = format!("Cargo.toml [{}] {}", dep.table, dep.name);
   678	        if denied(&dep.name) {
   679	            violations.push(format!("{at}: denied crate name"));
   680	        }
   681	        if let Some(package) = dep.package.filter(|package| denied(package)) {
   682	            violations.push(format!("{at}: renames denied crate `{package}`"));
   683	        }
   684	        if let Some(path) = dep.path {
   685	            if normalize(&crate_dir.join(&path)).starts_with(&pipeline) {
   686	                violations.push(format!("{at}: path `{path}` points into pipeline/"));
   687	            }
   688	        }
   689	    }
   690	    violations
   691	}
   692	
   693	// --- The lock -------------------------------------------------------------------
   694	
   695	struct Package {
   696	    name: String,
   697	    version: String,
   698	    /// `None` for a path package: the room itself, or a path dependency.
   699	    source: Option<String>,
   700	    /// As the lock writes them: `name`, `name version` or `name version (source)`.
   701	    /// A `replace = "name version (source)"` field, which `[replace]` writes on
   702	    /// the package it redirects, is one more: Cargo builds that package instead.
   703	    deps: Vec<String>,
   704	}
   705	
   706	fn lock_packages(lock: &str) -> Vec<Package> {
   707	    let lock = parse_toml(lock);
   708	    let text = |package: &Toml, key: &str| package.get(key).and_then(Toml::str).map(String::from);
   709	    lock.get("package")
   710	        .map_or(&[][..], Toml::items)
   711	        .iter()
   712	        .map(|package| Package {
   713	            name: text(package, "name").unwrap_or_default(),
   714	            version: text(package, "version").unwrap_or_default(),
   715	            source: text(package, "source"),
   716	            deps: package
   717	                .get("dependencies")
   718	                .map_or(&[][..], Toml::items)
   719	                .iter()
   720	                .filter_map(Toml::str)
   721	                .map(String::from)
   722	                .chain(text(package, "replace"))
   723	                .collect(),
   724	        })
   725	        .collect()
   726	}
   727	
   728	/// A lock source without the commit a git source is pinned to. Cargo writes a
   729	/// package's source as `git+URL?query#commit` and a dependency line's as
   730	/// `(git+URL?query)`; the URL and its `?branch=`/`?tag=`/`?rev=` still tell
   731	/// two git sources apart.
   732	fn unpinned(source: &str) -> &str {
   733	    source.split_once('#').map_or(source, |(source, _)| source)
   734	}
   735	
   736	/// The packages a lock entry's dependency line can mean. The lock adds the
   737	/// version, then the source, only when the shorter form would be ambiguous.
   738	fn resolve<'a>(packages: &'a [Package], dep: &str) -> Vec<usize> {
   739	    let (spec, source) = match dep.split_once(" (") {
   740	        Some((spec, source)) => (spec, Some(source.trim_end_matches(')'))),
   741	        None => (dep, None),
   742	    };
   743	    let mut words = spec.split_whitespace();
   744	    let name = words.next().unwrap_or_default();
   745	    let version = words.next();
   746	    (0..packages.len())
   747	        .filter(|&index| {
   748	            let candidate: &'a Package = &packages[index];
   749	            candidate.name == name
   750	                && version.is_none_or(|version| candidate.version == version)
   751	                && source.is_none_or(|source| {
   752	                    candidate.source.as_deref().map(unpinned) == Some(unpinned(source))
   753	                })
   754	        })
   755	        .collect()
   756	}
   757	
   758	/// Every package the room reaches in its lock, the room included.
   759	fn room_subgraph(packages: &[Package]) -> Vec<&Package> {
   760	    let root = packages
   761	        .iter()
   762	        .position(|package| package.name == "room" && package.source.is_none())
   763	        .expect("the lock holds the room crate itself");
   764	    let mut seen = BTreeSet::new();
   765	    let mut stack = vec![root];
   766	    while let Some(index) = stack.pop() {
   767	        if !seen.insert(index) {
   768	            continue;
   769	        }
   770	        let package = &packages[index];
   771	        for dep in &package.deps {
   772	            let found = resolve(packages, dep);
   773	            assert!(
   774	                !found.is_empty(),
   775	                "Cargo.lock lists `{dep}` under {} but holds no such package",
   776	                package.name
   777	            );
   778	            stack.extend(found);
   779	        }
   780	    }
   781	    seen.into_iter().map(|index| &packages[index]).collect()
   782	}
   783	
   784	fn lock_violations(lock: &str) -> Vec<String> {
   785	    let packages = lock_packages(lock);
   786	    let mut violations = Vec::new();
   787	    for package in room_subgraph(&packages) {
   788	        let at = format!("Cargo.lock {} {}", package.name, package.version);
   789	        if denied(&package.name) {
   790	            violations.push(format!("{at}: denied crate in the room's subgraph"));
   791	        }
   792	        if package.source.is_none() && package.name != "room" {
   793	            violations.push(format!("{at}: a path package other than the room"));
   794	        }
   795	    }
   796	    violations
   797	}
   798	
   799	// --- The source -----------------------------------------------------------------
   800	//
   801	// A tokenizer that knows what Rust does not read as code: line and nested block
   802	// comments, string, byte and C string literals (raw ones too), character
   803	// literals and lifetimes. What is left is identifiers (raw ones unwrapped),
   804	// `::`, braces, `;`, `,` and single punctuation, which is all the import reader
   805	// needs.
   806	
   807	#[derive(Debug, PartialEq)]
   808	enum Tok {
   809	    /// An identifier or keyword; `true` when written raw (`r#name`), which is
   810	    /// never a keyword.
   811	    Ident(String, bool),
   812	    PathSep,
   813	    Open,
   814	    Close,
   815	    Semi,
   816	    Comma,
   817	    /// Any other single character: `#`, `!`, `[`, `]`, `(`, `<`, …
   818	    Punct(char),
   819	}
   820	
   821	fn is_ident_char(c: char) -> bool {
   822	    c.is_alphanumeric() || c == '_'
   823	}
   824	
   825	/// Past a `"…"` body whose opening quote is just before `at`.
   826	fn skip_quoted(text: &[char], mut at: usize) -> usize {
   827	    while at < text.len() {
   828	        match text[at] {
   829	            '\\' => at += 2,
   830	            '"' => return at + 1,
   831	            _ => at += 1,
   832	        }
   833	    }
   834	    at
   835	}
   836	
   837	/// Past a raw string body whose opening `"` is just before `at`.
   838	fn skip_raw(text: &[char], mut at: usize, hashes: usize) -> usize {
   839	    while at < text.len() {
   840	        if text[at] == '"' && (1..=hashes).all(|k| text.get(at + k) == Some(&'#')) {
   841	            return at + 1 + hashes;
   842	        }
   843	        at += 1;
   844	    }
   845	    at
   846	}
   847	
   848	/// Past a character literal or a lifetime that starts with the `'` at `at`.
   849	fn skip_tick(text: &[char], at: usize) -> usize {
   850	    if text.get(at + 1) == Some(&'\\') {
   851	        let mut end = at + 3;
   852	        while end < text.len() && text[end] != '\'' {
   853	            end += 1;
   854	        }
   855	        return end + 1;
   856	    }
   857	    if text.get(at + 2) == Some(&'\'') {
   858	        return at + 3;
   859	    }
   860	    let mut end = at + 1;
   861	    while end < text.len() && is_ident_char(text[end]) {
   862	        end += 1;
   863	    }
   864	    end
   865	}
   866	
   867	fn tokens(source: &str) -> Vec<Tok> {
   868	    let text: Vec<char> = source.chars().collect();
   869	    let mut out = Vec::new();
   870	    let mut at = 0;
   871	    while at < text.len() {
   872	        let c = text[at];
   873	        let next = text.get(at + 1).copied();
   874	        if c.is_whitespace() {
   875	            at += 1;
   876	        } else if c == '/' && next == Some('/') {
   877	            while at < text.len() && text[at] != '\n' {
   878	                at += 1;
   879	            }
   880	        } else if c == '/' && next == Some('*') {
   881	            let mut depth = 0;
   882	            while at < text.len() {
   883	                if text[at] == '/' && text.get(at + 1) == Some(&'*') {
   884	                    depth += 1;
   885	                    at += 2;
   886	                } else if text[at] == '*' && text.get(at + 1) == Some(&'/') {
   887	                    depth -= 1;
   888	                    at += 2;
   889	                    if depth == 0 {
   890	                        break;
   891	                    }
   892	                } else {
   893	                    at += 1;
   894	                }
   895	            }
   896	        } else if c == '"' {
   897	            at = skip_quoted(&text, at + 1);
   898	        } else if c == '\'' {
   899	            at = skip_tick(&text, at);
   900	        } else if c.is_alphabetic() || c == '_' {
   901	            let start = at;
   902	            while at < text.len() && is_ident_char(text[at]) {
   903	                at += 1;
   904	            }
   905	            let word: String = text[start..at].iter().collect();
   906	            let after = text.get(at).copied();
   907	            if matches!(word.as_str(), "b" | "c") && after == Some('"') {
   908	                at = skip_quoted(&text, at + 1);
   909	                continue;
   910	            }
   911	            if word == "b" && after == Some('\'') {
   912	                at = skip_tick(&text, at);
   913	                continue;
   914	            }
   915	            if matches!(word.as_str(), "r" | "br" | "cr") && matches!(after, Some('"' | '#')) {
   916	                let mut end = at;
   917	                while text.get(end) == Some(&'#') {
   918	                    end += 1;
   919	                }
   920	                let hashes = end - at;
   921	                if text.get(end) == Some(&'"') {
   922	                    at = skip_raw(&text, end + 1, hashes);
   923	                    continue;
   924	                }
   925	                if word == "r"
   926	                    && hashes == 1
   927	                    && text
   928	                        .get(end)
   929	                        .is_some_and(|c| c.is_alphabetic() || *c == '_')
   930	                {
   931	                    let start = end;
   932	                    while end < text.len() && is_ident_char(text[end]) {
   933	                        end += 1;
   934	                    }
   935	                    out.push(Tok::Ident(text[start..end].iter().collect(), true));
   936	                    at = end;
   937	                    continue;
   938	                }
   939	            }
   940	            out.push(Tok::Ident(word, false));
   941	        } else if c.is_ascii_digit() {
   942	            while at < text.len() && is_ident_char(text[at]) {
   943	                at += 1;
   944	            }
   945	        } else {
   946	            at += 1;
   947	            out.push(match c {
   948	                ':' if next == Some(':') => {
   949	                    at += 1;
   950	                    Tok::PathSep
   951	                }
   952	                '{' => Tok::Open,
   953	                '}' => Tok::Close,
   954	                ';' => Tok::Semi,
   955	                ',' => Tok::Comma,
   956	                _ => Tok::Punct(c),
   957	            });
   958	        }
   959	    }
   960	    out
   961	}
   962	
   963	fn keyword(token: Option<&Tok>, word: &str) -> bool {
   964	    matches!(token, Some(Tok::Ident(name, false)) if name == word)
   965	}
   966	
   967	/// The root of each path in a use tree, and whether it was written absolute
   968	/// (`::name`, which in Rust 2018 and later is always an external crate).
   969	fn use_roots(tree: &[Tok], absolute: bool) -> Vec<(String, bool)> {
   970	    match tree.first() {
   971	        Some(Tok::PathSep) => use_roots(&tree[1..], true),
   972	        Some(Tok::Open) => {
   973	            let mut out = Vec::new();
   974	            let mut depth = 0;
   975	            let mut start = 1;
   976	            for (index, token) in tree.iter().enumerate().skip(1) {
   977	                match token {
   978	                    Tok::Open => depth += 1,
   979	                    Tok::Close if depth == 0 => {
   980	                        out.extend(use_roots(&tree[start..index], absolute));
   981	                        break;
   982	                    }
   983	                    Tok::Close => depth -= 1,
   984	                    Tok::Comma if depth == 0 => {
   985	                        out.extend(use_roots(&tree[start..index], absolute));
   986	                        start = index + 1;
   987	                    }
   988	                    _ => {}
   989	                }
   990	            }
   991	            out
   992	        }
   993	        Some(Tok::Ident(name, _)) => vec![(name.clone(), absolute)],
   994	        _ => Vec::new(),
   995	    }
   996	}
   997	
   998	/// One crate-or-module root a file imports.
   999	struct Import {
  1000	    /// The scope the import sits in (see [`FileImports::parent`]).
  1001	    scope: usize,
  1002	    root: String,
  1003	    /// Written `::root`, or by `extern crate`: always an external crate.
  1004	    external: bool,
  1005	    keyword: &'static str,
  1006	}
  1007	
  1008	/// A file's imports and the module names it declares, by scope.
  1009	struct FileImports {
  1010	    found: Vec<Import>,
  1011	    /// `(scope, name)` for each `mod name;` or `mod name { … }`.
  1012	    declared: BTreeSet<(usize, String)>,
  1013	    /// Each scope's enclosing scope. Scope 0 is the file; every brace opens
  1014	    /// one more. A block sees the names its enclosing blocks declare, up to its
  1015	    /// module, so a block's parent is the scope around it. A module (the file,
  1016	    /// or an inline `mod name { … }`) has none: Rust does not look past it
  1017	    /// without `super::`.
  1018	    parent: Vec<Option<usize>>,
  1019	    /// Each inline module's scope, and the `(scope, name)` that declares it,
  1020	    /// whether or not the declaration counts.
  1021	    modules: BTreeMap<usize, (usize, String)>,
  1022	    /// The declarations written `mod name;`, which load a file.
  1023	    loads: BTreeSet<(usize, String)>,
  1024	    /// The file starts `#![cfg(…)]` (or `cfg_attr`, `path`), so the module it
  1025	    /// holds may be absent.
  1026	    conditional: bool,
  1027	}
  1028	
  1029	impl FileImports {
  1030	    /// Whether `name` is a module that `scope` can see: declared in it, or in a
  1031	    /// scope around it within the same module.
  1032	    fn declares(&self, scope: usize, name: &str) -> bool {
  1033	        let mut at = Some(scope);
  1034	        while let Some(scope) = at {
  1035	            if self.declared.contains(&(scope, name.to_string())) {
  1036	                return true;
  1037	            }
  1038	            at = self.parent[scope];
  1039	        }
  1040	        false

 succeeded in 0ms:
  1040	        false
  1041	    }
  1042	}
  1043	
  1044	fn imports(source: &str) -> FileImports {
  1045	    let tokens = tokens(source);
  1046	    let mut file = FileImports {
  1047	        found: Vec::new(),
  1048	        declared: BTreeSet::new(),
  1049	        parent: vec![None],
  1050	        modules: BTreeMap::new(),
  1051	        loads: BTreeSet::new(),
  1052	        conditional: false,
  1053	    };
  1054	    let mut scopes = vec![0];
  1055	    // The next item carries a `#[cfg…]`, `#[cfg_attr…]` or `#[path…]`. Only an
  1056	    // unconditional `mod` makes a `use` of its name local: a conditional one
  1057	    // may be absent exactly when the `use` is compiled.
  1058	    let mut conditional = false;
  1059	    let mut at = 0;
  1060	    while at < tokens.len() {
  1061	        let here = *scopes.last().expect("the file scope is never popped");
  1062	        if let Some((end, inner, gated)) = attribute(&tokens, at) {
  1063	            if gated && !inner {
  1064	                conditional = true;
  1065	            } else if gated && here == 0 {
  1066	                // An inner `#![cfg…]` gates the module it sits in: this file's,
  1067	                file.conditional = true;
  1068	            } else if let Some(declaration) = file.modules.get(&here).filter(|_| gated) {
  1069	                // or an inline one's.
  1070	                file.declared.remove(declaration);
  1071	            }
  1072	            at = end;
  1073	            continue;
  1074	        }
  1075	        if keyword(tokens.get(at), "mod") {
  1076	            let counts = !conditional;
  1077	            conditional = false;
  1078	            if let Some(Tok::Ident(name, _)) = tokens.get(at + 1) {
  1079	                let declaration = (here, name.clone());
  1080	                match tokens.get(at + 2) {
  1081	                    Some(Tok::Semi) => {
  1082	                        if counts {
  1083	                            file.loads.insert(declaration.clone());
  1084	                            file.declared.insert(declaration);
  1085	                        }
  1086	                        at += 3;
  1087	                        continue;
  1088	                    }
  1089	                    Some(Tok::Open) => {
  1090	                        let scope = file.parent.len();
  1091	                        if counts {
  1092	                            file.declared.insert(declaration.clone());
  1093	                        }
  1094	                        file.modules.insert(scope, declaration);
  1095	                        scopes.push(scope);
  1096	                        file.parent.push(None);
  1097	                        at += 3;
  1098	                        continue;
  1099	                    }
  1100	                    _ => {}
  1101	                }
  1102	            }
  1103	        } else if keyword(tokens.get(at), "use")
  1104	            && !matches!(tokens.get(at + 1), Some(Tok::Punct(_)))
  1105	        {
  1106	            conditional = false;
  1107	            // `use<'a>` in `impl Trait + use<'a>` is not an import, and is the
  1108	            // only `use` followed by punctuation.
  1109	            let end = tokens[at + 1..]
  1110	                .iter()
  1111	                .position(|token| *token == Tok::Semi)
  1112	                .map_or(tokens.len(), |offset| at + 1 + offset);
  1113	            for (root, external) in use_roots(&tokens[at + 1..end], false) {
  1114	                file.found.push(Import {
  1115	                    scope: here,
  1116	                    root,
  1117	                    external,
  1118	                    keyword: "use",
  1119	                });
  1120	            }
  1121	            at = end + 1;
  1122	            continue;
  1123	        } else if keyword(tokens.get(at), "extern") && keyword(tokens.get(at + 1), "crate") {
  1124	            conditional = false;
  1125	            if let Some(Tok::Ident(name, _)) = tokens.get(at + 2) {
  1126	                file.found.push(Import {
  1127	                    scope: here,
  1128	                    root: name.clone(),
  1129	                    external: true,
  1130	                    keyword: "extern crate",
  1131	                });
  1132	                at += 3;
  1133	                continue;
  1134	            }
  1135	        } else if tokens[at] == Tok::Open {
  1136	            conditional = false;
  1137	            scopes.push(file.parent.len());
  1138	            file.parent.push(Some(here));
  1139	        } else if tokens[at] == Tok::Close {
  1140	            conditional = false;
  1141	            if scopes.len() > 1 {
  1142	                scopes.pop();
  1143	            }
  1144	        } else if matches!(tokens[at], Tok::Semi | Tok::Comma) {
  1145	            conditional = false;
  1146	        }
  1147	        at += 1;
  1148	    }
  1149	    file
  1150	}
  1151	
  1152	/// The attribute starting at `at` (`#[…]`, or inner `#![…]`): where it ends,
  1153	/// whether it is inner, and whether it can make its item absent or come from
  1154	/// another file (`cfg`, `cfg_attr`, `path`).
  1155	fn attribute(tokens: &[Tok], at: usize) -> Option<(usize, bool, bool)> {
  1156	    if tokens.get(at) != Some(&Tok::Punct('#')) {
  1157	        return None;
  1158	    }
  1159	    let inner = tokens.get(at + 1) == Some(&Tok::Punct('!'));
  1160	    let open = at + 1 + usize::from(inner);
  1161	    if tokens.get(open) != Some(&Tok::Punct('[')) {
  1162	        return None;
  1163	    }
  1164	    let mut depth = 0;
  1165	    for (index, token) in tokens.iter().enumerate().skip(open) {
  1166	        match token {
  1167	            Tok::Punct('[') => depth += 1,
  1168	            Tok::Punct(']') => {
  1169	                depth -= 1;
  1170	                if depth == 0 {
  1171	                    // Raw or not: `#[r#cfg(…)]` is `#[cfg(…)]`.
  1172	                    let conditional = tokens[open..index].iter().any(|token| {
  1173	                        matches!(token, Tok::Ident(name, _)
  1174	                            if matches!(name.as_str(), "cfg" | "cfg_attr" | "path"))
  1175	                    });
  1176	                    return Some((index + 1, inner, conditional));
  1177	                }
  1178	            }
  1179	            _ => {}
  1180	        }
  1181	    }
  1182	    None
  1183	}
  1184	
  1185	/// What is wrong with one source file, and the denied names it was allowed to
  1186	/// `use` because its own module declares them.
  1187	///
  1188	/// A `use` root that its scope can see declared with `mod` is that module,
  1189	/// which is how Rust resolves it: `server/routes/mod.rs` declares
  1190	/// `mod pipeline;` (the room's admin route for the pipeline's pushes) and then
  1191	/// `use pipeline::admin_routes;`. A `mod` inside a block is seen only inside
  1192	/// that block, and a conditional `mod` (see [`imports`]) is not counted, nor
  1193	/// (see [`scan_files`]) is a `mod name;` whose file gates itself. An absolute
  1194	/// `::name` and `extern crate` always name a crate, so they get no such reading.
  1195	fn file_violations(file: &str, scanned: &FileImports) -> (Vec<String>, BTreeSet<String>) {
  1196	    let mut violations = Vec::new();
  1197	    let mut local = BTreeSet::new();
  1198	    for import in &scanned.found {
  1199	        if !denied(&import.root) {
  1200	            continue;
  1201	        }
  1202	        if !import.external && scanned.declares(import.scope, &import.root) {
  1203	            local.insert(import.root.clone());
  1204	            continue;
  1205	        }
  1206	        violations.push(format!(
  1207	            "{file}: `{}` of denied crate `{}`",
  1208	            import.keyword, import.root
  1209	        ));
  1210	    }
  1211	    (violations, local)
  1212	}
  1213	
  1214	/// One file on its own, as the planted cases use it.
  1215	fn source_violations(file: &str, source: &str) -> (Vec<String>, BTreeSet<String>) {
  1216	    file_violations(file, &imports(source))
  1217	}
  1218	
  1219	struct SourceScan {
  1220	    files: usize,
  1221	    imports: usize,
  1222	    violations: Vec<String>,
  1223	    local: BTreeSet<String>,
  1224	}
  1225	
  1226	/// The files `mod name;` at `scope` in the file at `path` can load: `name.rs`
  1227	/// or `name/mod.rs`, inside each inline module around it, under the declaring
  1228	/// file's own directory (a `mod.rs` or a crate root such as `lib.rs`) or, for
  1229	/// any other file, the directory named for it. `None` when an inline module
  1230	/// around it is itself conditional, which may carry a `#[path]` that moves it.
  1231	fn module_files(
  1232	    path: &Path,
  1233	    file: &FileImports,
  1234	    (scope, name): &(usize, String),
  1235	) -> Option<Vec<PathBuf>> {
  1236	    let mut inline = Vec::new();
  1237	    let mut at = Some(*scope);
  1238	    while let Some(scope) = at {
  1239	        match file.modules.get(&scope) {
  1240	            Some(declaration) => {
  1241	                if !file.declared.contains(declaration) {
  1242	                    return None;
  1243	                }
  1244	                inline.push(declaration.1.clone());
  1245	                at = Some(declaration.0);
  1246	            }
  1247	            None => at = file.parent[scope],
  1248	        }
  1249	    }
  1250	    let dir = path.parent().unwrap_or(Path::new(""));
  1251	    let mut bases = vec![dir.to_path_buf()];
  1252	    if path.file_name() != Some("mod.rs".as_ref()) {
  1253	        bases.extend(path.file_stem().map(|stem| dir.join(stem)));
  1254	    }
  1255	    Some(
  1256	        bases
  1257	            .into_iter()
  1258	            .flat_map(|base| {
  1259	                let dir = inline
  1260	                    .iter()
  1261	                    .rev()
  1262	                    .fold(base, |dir, module| dir.join(module));
  1263	                [
  1264	                    dir.join(format!("{name}.rs")),
  1265	                    dir.join(name).join("mod.rs"),
  1266	                ]
  1267	            })
  1268	            .collect(),
  1269	    )
  1270	}
  1271	
  1272	fn scan_source(crate_dir: &Path) -> SourceScan {
  1273	    let mut paths = Vec::new();
  1274	    rust_files(&crate_dir.join("src"), &mut paths);
  1275	    let files: Vec<(PathBuf, String)> = paths
  1276	        .into_iter()
  1277	        .map(|path| {
  1278	            let source = read(&path);
  1279	            (
  1280	                path.strip_prefix(crate_dir).unwrap_or(&path).to_path_buf(),
  1281	                source,
  1282	            )
  1283	        })
  1284	        .collect();
  1285	    scan_files(&files)
  1286	}
  1287	
  1288	/// Source files as paths within the crate and their text.
  1289	fn scan_files(files: &[(PathBuf, String)]) -> SourceScan {
  1290	    let mut scanned: Vec<FileImports> = files.iter().map(|(_, source)| imports(source)).collect();
  1291	    // A module file that gates itself makes the `mod name;` that loads it
  1292	    // conditional, as does one whose file this cannot place.
  1293	    let gated: BTreeSet<PathBuf> = files
  1294	        .iter()
  1295	        .zip(&scanned)
  1296	        .filter(|(_, file)| file.conditional)
  1297	        .map(|((path, _), _)| path.clone())
  1298	        .collect();
  1299	    for ((path, _), file) in files.iter().zip(&mut scanned) {
  1300	        let conditional: Vec<(usize, String)> = file
  1301	            .loads
  1302	            .iter()
  1303	            .filter(|declaration| {
  1304	                module_files(path, file, declaration)
  1305	                    .is_none_or(|candidates| candidates.iter().any(|path| gated.contains(path)))
  1306	            })
  1307	            .cloned()
  1308	            .collect();
  1309	        for declaration in conditional {
  1310	            file.declared.remove(&declaration);
  1311	        }
  1312	    }
  1313	    let mut scan = SourceScan {
  1314	        files: files.len(),
  1315	        imports: 0,
  1316	        violations: Vec::new(),
  1317	        local: BTreeSet::new(),
  1318	    };
  1319	    for ((path, _), file) in files.iter().zip(&scanned) {
  1320	        scan.imports += file.found.len();
  1321	        let (violations, local) = file_violations(&path.display().to_string(), file);
  1322	        scan.violations.extend(violations);
  1323	        scan.local.extend(local);
  1324	    }
  1325	    scan
  1326	}
  1327	
  1328	fn refuse(violations: &[String]) {
  1329	    assert!(
  1330	        violations.is_empty(),
  1331	        "{WHY}:\n  {}",
  1332	        violations.join("\n  ")
  1333	    );
  1334	}
