  1354	/// The three checks above pass vacuously if their readers see nothing. This
  1355	/// holds each reader to things known to be there.
  1356	#[test]
  1357	fn the_readers_see_every_section_they_check() {
  1358	    let dir = crate_dir();
  1359	    let manifest = parse_toml(&read(&dir.join("Cargo.toml")));
  1360	    for (key, _) in manifest.entries() {
  1361	        assert!(
  1362	            KNOWN_ROOT_KEYS.contains(&key.as_str()),
  1363	            "Cargo.toml has a `{key}` section this test does not know; \
  1364	             read it here if it can declare a dependency"
  1365	        );
  1366	    }
  1367	    for (cfg, target) in manifest.get("target").map_or(&[][..], Toml::entries) {
  1368	        for (key, _) in target.entries() {
  1369	            assert!(
  1370	                DEPENDENCY_KINDS.contains(&key.as_str()),
  1371	                "Cargo.toml [target.{cfg}] has a `{key}` table this test does not read"
  1372	            );
  1373	        }
  1374	    }
  1375	    let deps = manifest_deps(&manifest);
  1376	    let declared = |table: &str, name: &str| {
  1377	        deps.iter()
  1378	            .any(|dep| dep.table == table && dep.name == name)
  1379	    };
  1380	    assert!(
  1381	        declared("dependencies", "axum"),
  1382	        "[dependencies] axum was not read"
  1383	    );
  1384	    assert!(
  1385	        declared("dependencies", "tokio-tungstenite"),
  1386	        "the optional, feature-gated tokio-tungstenite was not read"
  1387	    );
  1388	    assert!(
  1389	        declared("dev-dependencies", "tower"),
  1390	        "[dev-dependencies] tower was not read"
  1391	    );
  1392	
  1393	    let packages = lock_packages(&read(&dir.join("Cargo.lock")));
  1394	    let reached = room_subgraph(&packages);
  1395	    for name in ["axum", "tokio-tungstenite", "tower", "ring"] {
  1396	        assert!(
  1397	            reached.iter().any(|package| package.name == name),
  1398	            "the room's lock subgraph is missing {name}"
  1399	        );
  1400	    }
  1401	    assert_eq!(
  1402	        reached.len(),
  1403	        packages.len(),
  1404	        "the room is the lock's only root, so it should reach every package"
  1405	    );
  1406	
  1407	    let scan = scan_source(&dir);
  1408	    assert!(scan.files > 10, "read {} source files", scan.files);
  1409	    assert!(scan.imports > 100, "read {} imports", scan.imports);
  1410	    assert!(
  1411	        scan.local.contains("pipeline"),
  1412	        "the scan should see the room's own `mod pipeline` route and its `use`"
  1413	    );
  1414	}
  1415	
  1416	#[test]
  1417	fn the_denylist_matches_crate_names_not_the_rooms_folder() {
  1418	    for name in [
  1419	        "anthropic",
  1420	        "async-openai",
  1421	        "openai_api_rust",
  1422	        "llm",
  1423	        "llm-chain",
  1424	        "generator",
  1425	        "miri",
  1426	        "cargo-miri",
  1427	        "pipeline",
  1428	        "popquiz",
  1429	        "rustc_driver",
  1430	        "rustc_interface",
  1431	    ] {
  1432	        assert!(denied(name), "{name} should be denied");
  1433	    }
  1434	    for name in [
  1435	        "room",
  1436	        "getrandom",
  1437	        "generic-array",
  1438	        "wit-bindgen",
  1439	        "axum",
  1440	        "tokio",
  1441	    ] {
  1442	        assert!(!denied(name), "{name} should be allowed");
  1443	    }
  1444	    // The folder is `popquiz/`; nothing above reads it. The crate is `room`.
  1445	    assert!(crate_dir().ends_with("popquiz"));
  1446	    assert!(!denied("room"));
  1447	}
  1448	
  1449	/// The real manifest with `extra` added straight after its first `header` line.
  1450	fn planted(manifest: &str, header: &str, extra: &str) -> String {
  1451	    let line = format!("{header}\n");
  1452	    let out = manifest.replacen(&line, &format!("{line}{extra}\n"), 1);
  1453	    assert_ne!(out, manifest, "{header} is not in Cargo.toml");
  1454	    out
  1455	}
  1456	
  1457	/// The mutations named on PQ-47, and the variants a review found, planted in
  1458	/// the real text. On the real files M1 and M2 stop `cargo --locked` before any
  1459	/// test runs; here they reach the checks, so the checks stay able to refuse them.
  1460	#[test]
  1461	fn the_planted_mutations_are_refused() {
  1462	    let dir = crate_dir();
  1463	    let manifest = read(&dir.join("Cargo.toml"));
  1464	    let refused = |text: &str, expected: &str| {
  1465	        let found = manifest_violations(&dir, text);
  1466	        assert!(
  1467	            found.iter().any(|violation| violation.contains(expected)),
  1468	            "expected a violation containing `{expected}`, got {found:?}"
  1469	        );
  1470	    };
  1470	    };
  1471	
  1472	    // M1: a path dependency on the pipeline.
  1473	    let m1 = planted(
  1474	        &manifest,
  1475	        "[dependencies]",
  1476	        "pipeline = { path = \"../pipeline\" }",
  1477	    );
  1478	    refused(&m1, "path `../pipeline` points into pipeline/");
  1479	    refused(&m1, "[dependencies] pipeline: denied crate name");
  1480	
  1481	    // M2: an optional, feature-gated LLM client.
  1482	    let m2 = planted(
  1483	        &planted(&manifest, "[features]", "llm = [\"dep:anthropic\"]"),
  1484	        "[dependencies]",
  1485	        "anthropic = { version = \"0.1\", optional = true }",
  1486	    );
  1487	    refused(&m2, "[dependencies] anthropic: denied crate name");
  1488	
  1489	    // Manifest variants: dotted keys, quoted keys, escaped and multi-line
  1490	    // strings, dotted and quoted headers, patch and replace.
  1491	    for (extra_header, extra, expected) in [
  1492	        (
  1493	            "[dependencies]",
  1494	            "anthropic.version = \"0.1\"\nanthropic.optional = true",
  1495	            "] anthropic: denied",
  1496	        ),
  1497	        (
  1498	            "[dependencies]",
  1499	            "client = { \"package\" = \"openai\", version = \"0.1\" }",
  1500	            "`openai`",
  1501	        ),
  1502	        (
  1503	            "[dependencies]",
  1504	            "helper = { \"path\" = \"../pipeline\" }",
  1505	            "points into pipeline/",
  1506	        ),
  1507	        (
  1508	            "[dependencies]",
  1509	            "helper = { path = '../pipeline/src' }",
  1510	            "points into pipeline/",
  1511	        ),
  1512	        (
  1513	            "[dependencies]",
  1514	            "client = { package = \"op\\u0065nai\", version = \"0.1\" }",
  1515	            "`openai`",
  1516	        ),
  1517	        (
  1518	            "[dependencies]",
  1519	            "client = { package = \"\"\"openai\"\"\", version = \"0.1\" }",
  1520	            "`openai`",
  1521	        ),
  1522	        (
  1523	            "[dev-dependencies]",
  1524	            "client = { package = \"async-openai\", version = \"0.1\" }",
  1525	            "`async-openai`",
  1526	        ),
  1527	    ] {
  1528	        refused(&planted(&manifest, extra_header, extra), expected);
  1529	    }
  1530	    for (appended, expected) in [
  1531	        (
  1532	            "[target.'cfg(unix)'.build-dependencies.helper]\npackage = \"llm\"",
  1533	            "`llm`",
  1534	        ),
  1535	        (
  1536	            "[target.'cfg(any())'.\"build-dependencies\"]\nllm = \"0.1\"",
  1537	            "] llm: denied",
  1538	        ),
  1539	        (
  1540	            "[target . 'cfg(any())' . build-dependencies]\nllm = \"0.1\"",
  1541	            "] llm: denied",
  1542	        ),
  1543	        (
  1544	            "[patch.crates-io]\nopenai = { git = \"https://example.com/x\" }",
  1545	            "[patch.crates-io] openai",
  1546	        ),
  1547	        (
  1548	            "[replace]\n\"miri:0.1.0\" = { git = \"https://example.com/x\" }",
  1549	            "[replace] miri",
  1550	        ),
  1551	        // The other package ID spec forms a `[replace]` key can take.
  1552	        (
  1553	            "[replace]\n\"openai@0.1.0\" = { git = \"https://example.com/x\" }",
  1554	            "[replace] openai",
  1555	        ),
  1556	        (
  1557	            "[replace]\n\"https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index#openai@0.1.0\" = \
  1558	             { git = \"https://example.com/x\" }",
  1559	            "[replace] openai",
  1560	        ),
  1561	        (
  1562	            "[replace]\n\"registry+https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index#openai:0.1.0\" = \
  1563	             { git = \"https://example.com/x\" }",
  1564	            "[replace] openai",
  1565	        ),
  1566	        (
  1567	            "[replace]\n\"https://example.com/openai#0.1.0\" = { git = \"https://example.com/x\" }",
  1568	            "[replace] openai",
  1569	        ),
  1570	        (
  1571	            "[replace]\n\"git+https://example.com/x/openai.git?branch=main\" = \
  1572	             { git = \"https://example.com/x\" }",
  1573	            "[replace] openai",
  1574	        ),
  1575	        // Spellings the URL parser Cargo uses normalizes to the same name.
  1576	        (
  1577	            "[replace]\n'https://example.com/helpers\\openai#0.1.0' = { git = \"https://example.com/x\" }",
  1578	            "[replace] openai",
  1579	        ),
  1580	        (
  1581	            "[replace]\n\"https://example.com/openai/x/..#0.1.0\" = { git = \"https://example.com/x\" }",
  1582	            "[replace] openai",
  1583	        ),
  1584	        (
  1585	            "[replace]\n\"https://example.com/openai/%2E/#0.1.0\" = { git = \"https://example.com/x\" }",
  1586	            "[replace] openai",
  1587	        ),
  1588	        (
  1589	            "[replace]\n\"https://example.com/open\\tai#0.1.0\" = { git = \"https://example.com/x\" }",
  1590	            "[replace] openai",
  1591	        ),
  1592	        (
  1593	            "[replace]\n\"https://example.com/x#open\\nai@0.1.0\" = { git = \"https://example.com/x\" }",
  1594	            "[replace] openai",
  1595	        ),
  1596	    ] {
  1597	        refused(&format!("{manifest}\n{appended}\n"), expected);
  1598	    }
  1599	
  1600	    // Workspace inheritance: the spec is in `[workspace.dependencies]`, or in a
  1601	    // workspace above the room that this test cannot read.
  1602	    let inheriting = planted(
  1603	        &manifest,
  1604	        "[dependencies]",
  1605	        "client = { workspace = true, optional = true }",
  1606	    );
  1607	    refused(
  1608	        &format!(
  1609	            "{inheriting}\n[workspace.dependencies]\n\
  1610	             client = {{ package = \"openai\", version = \"0.1\" }}\n"
  1611	        ),
  1612	        "[workspace.dependencies] client: renames denied crate `openai`",
  1613	    );
  1614	    refused(
  1615	        &format!("{manifest}\n[workspace.dependencies]\nhelper = {{ path = \"../pipeline\" }}\n"),
  1616	        "[workspace.dependencies] helper: path `../pipeline` points into pipeline/",
  1617	    );
  1618	    // A name of its own, so a manifest that already inherits `client` from its
  1619	    // own workspace still shows this case.
  1620	    refused(
  1621	        &planted(
  1622	            &manifest,
  1623	            "[dependencies]",
  1624	            "pq47-inherited = { workspace = true }",
  1625	        ),
  1626	        "[dependencies] pq47-inherited: inherits from a workspace outside",
  1627	    );
  1628	
  1629	    // M3: an import the compiler never sees.
  1630	    let source = |text: &str| source_violations("planted.rs", text).0;
  1631	    assert_eq!(
  1632	        source("#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n").len(),
  1633	        1,
  1634	        "M3"
  1635	    );
  1636	
  1637	    // Source variants: layout, raw identifiers, absolute paths, nested modules.
  1638	    for text in [
  1639	        "#[cfg(any())]\nuse\n    openai::Client;\n",
  1640	        "#[cfg(any())]\nuse{openai::Client};\n",
  1641	        "fn f() {} #[cfg(any())] use openai::Client;\n",
  1642	        "use {std::fmt, ::openai::Client};\n",
  1643	        "use std::{\n    fmt,\n};\nuse anthropic\n    ::Messages;\n",
  1644	        "#[cfg(any())]\nuse r#openai::Client;\n",
  1645	        "extern crate rustc_driver;\n",
  1646	        "extern crate r#popquiz;\n",
  1647	        "mod popquiz;\nextern crate popquiz;\n",
  1648	        "mod pipeline;\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse ::pipeline::Client;\n",
  1649	        "mod inner {\n    mod openai;\n}\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1650	        "fn f() {\n    use generator::Gen;\n}\n",
  1651	        // A `mod` in a block is not seen outside it.
  1652	        "fn f() { mod openai {} }\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1653	        "fn f() {\n    { mod openai {} }\n    use openai::Client;\n}\n",
  1654	        "const _: () = { mod openai {} };\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1655	        // Nor is a module's `mod` seen inside an inline module, without `super::`.
  1656	        "mod openai;\nmod inner {\n    use openai::Client;\n}\n",
  1657	        // A conditional `mod` may be absent exactly when the `use` is compiled.
  1658	        "#[cfg(not(feature = \"llm\"))]\nmod openai {}\n#[cfg(feature = \"llm\")]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1659	        "#[cfg(not(feature = \"llm\"))]\npub(crate) mod openai;\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1660	        "#[allow(dead_code)]\n#[cfg_attr(any(), cfg(any()))]\nmod openai;\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1661	        "#[path = \"elsewhere.rs\"]\nmod openai;\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1662	        "mod openai {\n    #![cfg(not(feature = \"llm\"))]\n}\n#[cfg(feature = \"llm\")]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1663	        // The same gates spelled as raw identifiers.
  1664	        "#[r#cfg(not(feature = \"llm\"))]\nmod openai {}\n#[cfg(feature = \"llm\")]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1665	        "mod openai {\n    #![r#cfg(not(feature = \"llm\"))]\n}\n#[cfg(feature = \"llm\")]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1666	        "#[r#cfg_attr(any(), r#cfg(any()))]\nmod openai;\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1667	        "#[r#path = \"elsewhere.rs\"]\nmod openai;\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1668	    ] {
  1669	        assert_eq!(source(text).len(), 1, "{text}");
  1670	    }
  1671	
  1672	    // The room's own `mod pipeline` is local where it is seen, and only there.
  1673	    for text in [
  1674	        "mod pipeline;\npub(crate) use pipeline::admin_routes;\n",
  1675	        "mod pipeline;\nfn f() {\n    use pipeline::admin_routes;\n}\n",
  1676	        "fn f() {\n    mod pipeline {}\n    use pipeline::admin_routes;\n}\n",
  1677	        "fn f() {\n    mod pipeline {}\n    {\n        use pipeline::admin_routes;\n    }\n}\n",
  1678	        // Attributes that cannot remove it, and a gate on the item before it.
  1679	        "#[allow(dead_code)]\npub(crate) mod pipeline;\nuse pipeline::admin_routes;\n",
  1680	        "#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {}\nmod pipeline;\nuse pipeline::admin_routes;\n",
  1681	        "#[cfg(test)]\nconst A: u8 = 1;\nmod pipeline;\nuse pipeline::admin_routes;\n",
  1682	        "#![cfg_attr(docsrs, feature(doc_cfg))]\nmod pipeline;\nuse pipeline::admin_routes;\n",
  1683	    ] {
  1684	        assert!(source(text).is_empty(), "{text}");
  1685	    }
  1686	    assert_eq!(source("pub(crate) use pipeline::admin_routes;\n").len(), 1);
  1687	
  1688	    // A `mod name;` whose file gates itself, as `name.rs` or `name/mod.rs`.
  1689	    let scan = |files: &[(&str, &str)]| {
  1690	        let files: Vec<(PathBuf, String)> = files
  1691	            .iter()
  1692	            .map(|(path, text)| (PathBuf::from(path), text.to_string()))
  1693	            .collect();
  1694	        scan_files(&files).violations
  1695	    };
  1696	    let routes = "mod pipeline;\nuse pipeline::admin_routes;\n";
  1697	    for module in ["src/pipeline.rs", "src/pipeline/mod.rs"] {
  1698	        assert_eq!(
  1699	            scan(&[
  1700	                ("src/lib.rs", routes),
  1701	                (module, "#![cfg(not(feature = \"llm\"))]\n")
  1702	            ])
  1703	            .len(),
  1704	            1,
  1705	            "{module}"
  1706	        );
  1707	        assert!(
  1708	            scan(&[("src/lib.rs", routes), (module, "#![allow(dead_code)]\n")]).is_empty(),
  1709	            "{module}"
  1710	        );
  1711	    }
  1712	    // The file is the one that `mod` loads, wherever it is declared: beside a
  1713	    // `mod.rs`, in the folder named for any other file, inside inline modules.
  1714	    let gate = "#![r#cfg(not(feature = \"llm\"))]\n";
  1715	    for files in [
  1716	        [
  1717	            ("src/server/routes/mod.rs", routes),
  1718	            ("src/server/routes/pipeline.rs", gate),
  1719	        ],
  1720	        [
  1721	            ("src/server/routes.rs", routes),
  1722	            ("src/server/routes/pipeline/mod.rs", gate),
  1723	        ],
  1724	        [
  1725	            (
  1726	                "src/lib.rs",
  1727	                "mod inner {\n    mod pipeline;\n    use pipeline::admin_routes;\n}\n",
  1728	            ),
  1729	            ("src/inner/pipeline.rs", gate),
  1730	        ],
  1731	    ] {
  1732	        assert_eq!(scan(&files).len(), 1, "{files:?}");
  1733	    }
  1734	    // An inline module that may move under `#[path]` leaves its file unplaced.
  1735	    assert_eq!(
  1736	        scan(&[(
  1737	            "src/lib.rs",
  1738	            "#[path = \"elsewhere\"]\nmod inner {\n    mod pipeline;\n    use pipeline::admin_routes;\n}\n"
  1739	        )])
  1740	        .len(),
  1741	        1
  1742	    );
  1743	    // A gated file of the same name elsewhere is another module.
  1744	    for elsewhere in [
  1745	        "src/unrelated/pipeline.rs",
  1746	        "src/server/pipeline.rs",
  1747	        "src/routes/pipeline/mod.rs",
  1748	    ] {
  1749	        assert!(
  1750	            scan(&[("src/server/routes/mod.rs", routes), (elsewhere, gate)]).is_empty(),
  1751	            "{elsewhere}"
  1752	        );
  1753	    }
  1754	}
  1755	
  1756	/// Text that only looks like a dependency or an import is not one.
  1757	#[test]
  1758	fn the_readers_do_not_mistake_text_for_code() {
  1759	    let dir = crate_dir();
  1760	    let manifest = read(&dir.join("Cargo.toml"));
  1761	    // Measured against the manifest's own result, so a real dependency planted
  1762	    // in it fails the manifest check alone, not this one too.
  1763	    let baseline = manifest_violations(&dir, &manifest);
  1764	    for (what, text) in [
  1765	        (
  1766	            "a multi-line string",
  1767	            planted(
  1768	                &manifest,
  1769	                "[package]",
  1770	                "metadata.example = \"\"\"\n[dependencies]\nopenai = \"0.1\"\n\"\"\"",
  1771	            ),
  1772	        ),
  1773	        (
  1774	            "a multi-line literal string",
  1775	            planted(
  1776	                &manifest,
  1777	                "[package]",
  1778	                "metadata.example = '''\n[dependencies]\nopenai = \"0.1\"\n'''",
  1779	            ),
  1780	        ),
  1781	        (
  1782	            "a comment",
  1783	            planted(&manifest, "[dependencies]", "# openai = \"0.1\""),
  1784	        ),
  1785	        (
  1786	            "an empty patch table",
  1787	            format!("{manifest}\n[patch.crates-io]\n"),
  1788	        ),
  1789	    ] {
  1790	        assert_eq!(manifest_violations(&dir, &text), baseline, "{what}");
  1791	    }
  1792	
  1793	    for text in [
  1794	        "/* an example:\nuse openai::Client;\n*/\n",
  1795	        "/* nested /* comments */\nuse openai::Client;\n*/\n",
  1796	        "// use openai::Client;\n",
  1797	        "const EXAMPLE: &str = r#\"\nuse openai::Client;\n\"#;\n",
  1798	        "const EXAMPLE: &str = \"\\\"\nuse openai::Client;\n\";\n",
  1799	        "const QUOTE: char = '\"';\nconst EXAMPLE: &str = \"use openai::Client;\";\n",
  1800	        "fn f<'a>(x: &'a str) -> impl Sized + use<'a> { x }\n",
  1801	        "fn pipeline_name() -> &'static str { \"pipeline\" }\n",
  1802	    ] {
  1803	        assert!(source_violations("text.rs", text).0.is_empty(), "{text}");
  1804	    }
  1805	}
  1806	
  1807	/// Cargo can lock two packages of one name and version from different sources,
  1808	/// and then names the source in each dependency line.
  1809	#[test]
  1810	fn the_lock_walk_tells_sources_apart() {
  1811	    let lock = r#"
  1812	version = 4
  1813	
  1814	[[package]]
  1815	name = "room"
  1816	version = "0.1.0"
  1817	dependencies = [
  1818	 "shim 1.0.0 (git+https://example.com/shim)",
  1819	]
  1820	
  1821	[[package]]
  1822	name = "shim"
  1823	version = "1.0.0"
  1824	source = "registry+https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index"
  1825	
  1826	[[package]]
  1827	name = "shim"
  1828	version = "1.0.0"
  1829	source = "git+https://example.com/shim"
  1830	dependencies = [
  1831	 "openai",
  1832	]
  1833	
  1834	[[package]]
  1835	name = "openai"
  1836	version = "0.1.0"
  1837	source = "registry+https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index"
  1838	"#;
  1839	    let found = lock_violations(lock);
  1840	    assert!(
  1841	        found.iter().any(|violation| violation.contains("openai")),
  1842	        "the git `shim` reaches openai: {found:?}"
  1843	    );
  1844	    let packages = lock_packages(lock);
  1845	    let reached: Vec<_> = room_subgraph(&packages)
  1846	        .iter()
  1847	        .map(|package| (package.name.clone(), package.source.clone()))
  1848	        .collect();
  1849	    assert_eq!(
  1850	        reached.len(),
  1851	        3,
  1852	        "room, the git shim and openai: {reached:?}"
  1853	    );
  1854	}
  1855	
  1856	/// The shape `cargo generate-lockfile` writes for one crate from two git
  1857	/// repositories (one on a branch): each package's source carries the commit it
  1858	/// is pinned to, and each dependency line names the source without it.
  1859	#[test]
  1860	fn the_lock_walk_matches_git_sources_without_their_commit() {
  1861	    let lock = r#"
  1862	version = 4
  1863	
  1864	[[package]]
  1865	name = "room"
  1866	version = "0.1.0"
  1867	dependencies = [
  1868	 "shim 1.0.0 (git+https://example.com/b?branch=dev)",
  1869	 "shim 1.0.0 (git+https://example.com/a)",
  1870	]
  1871	
  1872	[[package]]
  1873	name = "shim"
  1874	version = "1.0.0"
  1875	source = "git+https://example.com/b?branch=dev#84b81c3f13100567babfe14dea141f96d2d8f1f4"
  1876	dependencies = [
  1877	 "openai",
  1878	]
  1879	
  1880	[[package]]
  1881	name = "shim"
  1882	version = "1.0.0"
  1883	source = "git+https://example.com/a#2e4a07543fbfb2fbda07356922c0fa81f18b9bbb"
  1884	
  1885	[[package]]
  1886	name = "openai"
  1887	version = "0.1.0"
  1888	source = "registry+https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index"
  1889	"#;
  1890	    let packages = lock_packages(lock);
  1891	    let reached: Vec<_> = room_subgraph(&packages)
  1892	        .iter()
  1893	        .map(|package| (package.name.clone(), package.source.clone()))
  1894	        .collect();
  1895	    assert_eq!(reached.len(), 4, "room, both shims and openai: {reached:?}");
  1896	    assert!(
  1897	        lock_violations(lock)
  1898	            .iter()
  1899	            .any(|violation| violation.contains("openai")),
  1900	        "the branch `shim` reaches openai"
  1901	    );
  1902	    // The branch still tells the two repositories' sources apart.
  1903	    assert_eq!(
  1904	        resolve(
  1905	            &packages,
  1906	            "shim 1.0.0 (git+https://example.com/b?branch=dev)"
  1907	        ),
  1908	        vec![1]
  1909	    );
  1910	    assert_eq!(
  1911	        resolve(&packages, "shim 1.0.0 (git+https://example.com/a)"),
  1912	        vec![2]
  1913	    );
  1914	}
  1915	
  1916	/// The shape `cargo generate-lockfile` writes when `[replace]` redirects the
  1917	/// room's git `shim` to another repository: the replacement is no dependency
  1918	/// line, only a `replace` field on the package it stands in for.
  1919	#[test]
  1920	fn the_lock_walk_follows_replacements() {
  1921	    let lock = r#"
  1922	version = 4
  1923	
  1924	[[package]]
  1925	name = "room"
  1926	version = "0.1.0"
  1927	dependencies = [
  1928	 "shim 1.0.0 (git+https://example.com/a)",
  1929	]
  1930	
  1931	[[package]]
  1932	name = "shim"
  1933	version = "1.0.0"
  1934	source = "git+https://example.com/b?branch=dev#84b81c3f13100567babfe14dea141f96d2d8f1f4"
  1935	dependencies = [
  1936	 "openai",
  1937	]
  1938	
  1939	[[package]]
  1940	name = "shim"
  1941	version = "1.0.0"
  1942	source = "git+https://example.com/a#2e4a07543fbfb2fbda07356922c0fa81f18b9bbb"
  1943	replace = "shim 1.0.0 (git+https://example.com/b?branch=dev)"
  1944	
  1945	[[package]]
  1946	name = "openai"
  1947	version = "0.1.0"
  1948	source = "registry+https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index"
  1949	"#;
  1950	    let packages = lock_packages(lock);
  1951	    let reached = room_subgraph(&packages).len();
  1952	    assert_eq!(reached, 4, "room, both shims and openai");
  1953	    assert!(
  1954	        lock_violations(lock)
  1955	            .iter()
  1956	            .any(|violation| violation.contains("openai")),
  1957	        "the replacement `shim` reaches openai"
  1958	    );
  1959	}

 succeeded in 276ms:
commit e90be8bb332b730ccb1278689432d6cd6f746afd
Author:     Michelle Rojas <mrojas1028@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Tue Nov 3 09:25:21 2026 -0500
Commit:     Michelle Rojas <mrojas1028@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Tue Nov 3 09:25:21 2026 -0500

    Count only unconditional modules, and read every replace key form
    
    The third review found two more ways past the check. A `mod openai` under one
    `cfg` exempted a `use openai::…` under the opposite `cfg`, so the import
    reached the real crate whenever it was compiled; and a `[replace]` key written
    as `openai@0.1.0` or with a source URL was read as some other name.
    
    A module now makes an import local only when nothing can remove it: no outer
    `cfg`, `cfg_attr` or `path` attribute, no inner `#![cfg…]` in its body, and,
    for `mod name;`, no self-gating `name.rs` or `name/mod.rs`. Replace keys are
    read as Cargo's package ID specs.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

 popquiz/tests/dependencies.rs | 253 +++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++-----
 1 file changed, 227 insertions(+), 26 deletions(-)
commit f533c91b21fc085fd851a3d1fd4b599c7dc4b481
Author:     Michelle Rojas <mrojas1028@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Tue Nov 3 08:30:47 2026 -0500
Commit:     Michelle Rojas <mrojas1028@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Tue Nov 3 08:30:47 2026 -0500

    Keep block-local modules, workspace specs and git pins in view
    
    The second review found three gaps in the dependency check. A `mod` declared
    inside a function exempted a matching import outside it; dependencies
    inherited from `[workspace.dependencies]` were never read; and a lock with a
    git dependency made the walk panic, since Cargo pins a package's git source to
    a commit that its dependency lines leave out.
    
    Scopes now follow braces and stop at module boundaries, the workspace table is
    read like any other (and inheriting from a workspace above the room is
    refused), and git sources match without their commit but keep their branch.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

 popquiz/tests/dependencies.rs | 240 ++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++------
 1 file changed, 205 insertions(+), 35 deletions(-)
commit 92ae9e5bc84eca5b491b55807935eb5a4f4941c9
Author:     Michelle Rojas <mrojas1028@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Tue Nov 3 07:33:02 2026 -0500
Commit:     Michelle Rojas <mrojas1028@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Tue Nov 3 07:33:02 2026 -0500

    Parse the manifest, lock and source instead of matching lines
    
    The first review found layouts that hid a dependency from the line
    matchers: dotted and quoted keys, escaped or multi-line strings, quoted
    or spaced headers, imports split across lines or written `use{…}`, raw
    identifiers, and `::name` beside a local module of that name. It also
    found false alarms from comments, strings and an empty patch table.
    
    The manifest and lock now go through a small TOML parser and the source
    through a small Rust tokenizer, both in the test file. A module only
    excuses a `use` in the module that declares it, never for `::name` or
    `extern crate`. The lock walk tells packages apart by source. Each
    variant the review reproduced is a fixture.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

   132	the deck. Don't build decks. `mvp/` stays for its templates

   136	## House rules
   137	
   138	- **Never write down what a program prints.** Run it. Correct answers come from
   139	  `verified.json`, which is written by the verifier, never by hand (AC-7).
   140	- **The segment is one question, 3–5 minutes, scheduled last** (D-7, Story B10 /
   141	  AC-89…AC-92). Not a round. Anything that assumes several questions a night is
   142	  stale.
   143	- **Answer position is drawn uniformly from the date and nothing else**
   144	  (AC-23/23a/23b). Never balance it, never quota it, never avoid last meetup's
   145	  letter — every such rule converts an attendee's memory into a free
   146	  elimination, and enforced balance makes every fifth meetup certain. Uneven
   147	  counts and repeated letters are correct; do not "fix" them.

   211	licensed to reopen upstream artifacts — if a client answer contradicts one,
   212	the artifact is what's wrong.

   117	Still good and still load-bearing: the Experience State Contract, the pre-reveal
   118	answer-isolation requirements, the Discord authorization model, and the brand
   119	and accessibility contract.
   120	
   121	## Shape
   122	
   123	Two decoupled components:
   124	
   125	- **Offline question pipeline** — generate → pinned rustc + Miri verify →
   126	  dedupe → organizer review → a growing bank (public; see `bank/README.md`). No deadline.
   127	- **Live room app** — join by code, read code, answer, reveal, aggregate.
   128	
   190	  `web/test/agents-sync.test.js` (a regular file, never a symlink). After
   191	  editing `CLAUDE.md`, run `cp CLAUDE.md AGENTS.md`, or `just test` fails on
   192	  that one test.
   193	
   194	## Conventions
   195	
   196	- **`lib.rs`, `main.rs` and `mod.rs` are required, not banned** (ADR-0005,
   197	  "File names"). The crate root is `lib.rs`, the server binary is
   198	  `server/main.rs`, and every directory module is `<folder>/mod.rs`, never a
   199	  `<folder>.rs` beside its folder. Every other file is a noun named for what
   200	  it holds.
   201	- **Room commands take `--room <url>`.** `just schedule` and `just sync` need
   202	  it (`--no-push` replaces it on `schedule` when the room is down). `just
   203	  --list` shows these recipes only as `*ARGS`, so read the required arguments
   204	  in `docs/RUNBOOK.md` before running one, rather than learning them from a
   205	  failed run.

**Reviewing run state**
**Reviewing patch issues**
codex
FAIL: one Major source-scan bypass and three Minor defects remain, including an incomplete H fix and a valid replacement-lock panic exposed by I's traversal. The exact HEAD passes 10/10 dependency tests; the original F/G/H reproductions, M1–M3, and a freshly Cargo-generated ordinary replacement chain confirm the reported fixes, but the additional accepted-input reproductions above expose remaining failures. The branch changes only the new test file and does not deny the room by its folder name; the broader run completed 360 Rust tests with 4 ignored and 305 web tests, while pipeline and one Python-backed web test were blocked by uv-cache permissions rather than patch failures.

Full review comments:

- [P1] Major: Ignore macro tokens when declaring local modules — /Users/michellerojas/rust-nyc-pop-quiz-worktrees/pq-47-room-dep-denylist/popquiz/tests/dependencies.rs:1075-1079
  A compiler-accepted near variant of M3 still passes all 10 tests: append `const _: &str = stringify!(mod openai {});` followed by `#[cfg(any())] use openai::Client;` to `src/lib.rs`. The scanner treats the `mod` inside the macro's parentheses as a real declaration in the surrounding scope, so `file_violations` incorrectly exempts the separate import. I reproduced both successful rustc compilation and a 10/10 result against a temporary copy of the real source. This is not the documented limitation concerning imports generated by macros: the denied import is written literally outside the macro. Tokens inside a macro invocation must not establish local-module exemptions.

- [P2] Minor: Choose the actual module directory instead of both — /Users/michellerojas/rust-nyc-pop-quiz-worktrees/pq-47-room-dep-denylist/popquiz/tests/dependencies.rs:1250-1253
  H remains reproducible for crate roots because this adds both the declaring file's directory and its stem directory. For example, let `src/lib.rs` contain `mod pipeline; use pipeline::admin_routes;`, put the ordinary function in `src/pipeline/mod.rs`, and add an unrelated `src/lib/pipeline/mod.rs` containing `#![cfg(any())]`. Rustc accepts this layout and loads only `src/pipeline/mod.rs`, but the scanner also considers the unrelated file and reports the local import as denied. This follows the repository's crate-root and directory-module conventions ([AGENTS.md:196–199](../AGENTS.md#L196-L199)). Distinguish crate roots from other files before selecting a module base; checking both retains the false-positive mechanism that H was intended to remove.

- [P2] Minor: Remove only the lock edge's closing delimiter — /Users/michellerojas/rust-nyc-pop-quiz-worktrees/pq-47-room-dep-denylist/popquiz/tests/dependencies.rs:739-741
  A valid replacement source URL ending in `)` makes the new replacement traversal panic. I generated a real lock for a git `shim` replaced by another git repository named `shim-b)`; Cargo writes an edge ending in `(git+file:///…/shim-b))`, and `cargo check --offline --locked` succeeds. Here, `trim_end_matches(')')` removes both the edge delimiter and the URL's literal closing parenthesis, so `resolve` finds no matching package and `room_subgraph` panics. The same defect affects source-qualified ordinary dependency edges. Strip exactly one closing delimiter so valid Cargo-generated sources remain intact.

- [P2] Minor: Accept Cargo's optional leading UTF-8 BOM — /Users/michellerojas/rust-nyc-pop-quiz-worktrees/pq-47-room-dep-denylist/popquiz/tests/dependencies.rs:449-453
  Saving an otherwise valid `Cargo.toml` with a leading UTF-8 BOM causes this parser to panic at line 1 with `expected a key`, although Cargo accepts the manifest. I verified Cargo acceptance independently, then prepended U+FEFF to a temporary copy of the real manifest: four dependency tests panicked despite no dependency changing. The initial BOM reaches `key()` because neither parser initialization nor `blank()` consumes it. Remove an optional leading BOM before tokenization so an accepted manifest encoding does not break the guardrail suite.
